Any change after hashing goes to deviations.json with its reason.\\n\\nSHARED CONSTRUCTIONS (identical for every candidate)\\n  Layer index l in 0..L (0 = embedding); depth fraction f = l/L; layer at fraction f = round(f*L).\\n  Harm axis at layer l: diff-in-means of the 48 AdvBench minus the 48 Dolly EASY prompts, unit-normalised, float64. It is fitted on EASY and scored on HARD, so the fitting and scoring prompts never overlap.\\n  HARD positives = XSTest harmful + OR-Bench toxic (80); HARD negatives = XSTest benign twin + OR-Bench hard (80).\\n  Separability d(l): Cohen's d (pooled SD) of HARD positives versus negatives on the axis.\\n  TPR@x%FPR: threshold at the (1-x) quantile of the negative scores. n_neg = 80 gives a smallest step of 1/80. [CLAR-1] Wherever the strategy names 1% FPR with n_neg < 100, the statistic is the ZERO-FALSE-POSITIVE operating point (TPR above the maximum negative score). It is labelled TPR@0FP with n_neg printed, and 5% is always printed beside it. No threshold is ever interpolated.\\n  Cross-fit own-axis (C4, C7): 2-fold on HARD, folds grouped by twin pair or scenario so a harmful item and its benign twin never straddle folds, 20 seeded fold draws, scores standardised per fold before pooling.\\n  Per-prompt score distributions are saved for every checkpoint, layer and site (npz, fp16), not only means.\\n\\nTHE 14 CANDIDATES (every value is a per-checkpoint scalar computed inside one model)\\nEARLY / PROMPT SITE\\n  C1 restricted-budget recognition. Draw k/2 AdvBench + k/2 Dolly for k in {4, 8, 16}, with 200 seeded draws; fit the axis at f = 0.5 and score TPR@5%FPR on the 160 HARD prompts. The candidate value is the k = 16 mean (fixed now). The full k-curve with 5th-95th percentile spreads is printed, and no maximum over k is taken. Class: activation.\\n  C4 recognition onset depth. First l at which the cross-fit own-axis HARD AUROC is >= 0.80 for 2 consecutive layers, divided by L. Use iter-2's registered onset rule from H/prereg.json verbatim if it defines one, and record which rule was used. Class: activation.\\n  C13 request-axis Cohen's d: the peak over l of d(l), with its depth fraction printed as a companion. Labelled a RECOGNITION-type readout. The peak is part of the definition and is re-run inside every shuffled-label permutation, so clause (iii) prices the max.\\nLATE / RESPONSE SITE (A_resp; kept and slot_ok cells only)\\n  [CLAR-2] The response-site harm label is the REQUEST label (harmful vs benign_twin). The statistic is computed separately within each continuation condition (hazardous, benign) and averaged, so positives and negatives are compared at the same teacher-forced continuation text. Otherwise a response-site signal could simply be the continuation text, which is identical across models. The request x continuation 2x2 cell means are printed per checkpoint.\\n  Axis at a response position: diff-in-means at that position, cross-fitted 2-fold grouped by scenario (24 scenarios, 20 draws).\\n  C2 site gap = TPR@0FP(response position 0, f = 0.75) minus TPR@0FP(prompt site, f = 0.75), with TPR@5%FPR printed beside it. The prompt site uses the 80 XSTest items of A_prompt; the executor records how many of the 24 cell scenarios are among them, and the item-set mismatch goes in the table note.\\n  C9 response share = S_resp / (S_resp + S_prompt), where S is the mean cross-fit own-axis d over the deep half (f in [0.5, 1]); response position 0 versus the prompt tail.\\nDEPTH GEOMETRY (prompt site)\\n  C3 self-consistency = mean |cos| between the per-layer EASY axes of the shallow quarter (f in (0, 0.25]) and those of the deep half (f in [0.5, 1]).\\n  C6 fixed-axis accumulation = d along the axis FROZEN at f = 0.25, read at l = L, divided by d at f = 0.25.\\n  C7 area under the cross-fit own-axis d(l) curve against f (trapezoid). C6 and C7 are printed side by side for Base, Qwen3-4B, SafeRL, abliterated and CohenQu as the fixed-axis control: growth on the own axis but not the fixed axis indicates a layer-specific direction, not accumulation.\\n  C8 depth lag = (argmax over l of the HARD harm-conditioned refusal drive g(l) = mean over positives of (r_refusal - r_control)[l] minus the same over negatives) / L, minus C4. Class: activation(logit-lens).\\nDRIVE RESIDUAL\\n  C5 = max over l < L of g(l), with BL1 regressed out by OLS fitted on the TRAINING families only in each leave-one-family-out (LOFO) fold and the residual taken on the held-out family. Class: activation(logit-lens).\\nOTHER FAILURE DIRECTION AND GRADING\\n  C10 benign-side false alarm = the fraction of the 80 HARD negatives scoring above the EASY midpoint threshold on the f = 0.5 axis. Its primary outcome is OVER-REFUSAL; it is printed against HC as well.\\n  C11 = within-model Spearman between the f = 0.5 axis projection and ordinal harm severity 0-3. It needs a prompt-only CPU pass on at most 64 severity-labelled items per checkpoint (about 1-4.6 min per checkpoint from iter-2's timings), taken from the dataset dependency (art_1hlg) if it has a severity field or from the judge rubric (art_jn33) if that has one. If there is no severity source, the cache fetch fails, or the pass exceeds a 60-minute total cap, C11 is printed as NOT_EVALUATED with the reason; it is never dropped.\\nZERO-PROMPT\\n  C12 weight-only refusal loading. u = normalised mean(WU_ref) minus mean(WU_ctl), taken from the unembedding. e(l) = u' G_l u / (tr(G_l)/d), where G_l = gram/G_l.npy; confirm from src/wsummary.py which write matrix G is the Gram of, and record it. C12 = mean e over the deep half divided by mean e over the shallow quarter. Class: weight. It must beat the parent-free weight incumbents (X2 / X10_abs form and BL7_JORAK_A) on clause (i)'s statistic to count.\\nCOMBINATION\\n  C14: inside each LOFO fold, pick the early candidate (C1/C4/C13) and the late candidate (C2/C9) with the highest training-family rho, and fit a 2-feature Ridge(alpha=1) on the training families only. The held-out prediction is its score, and the chosen pair per fold is printed.\\n\\nINCUMBENT BARS, printed beside every row and never eligible: BL1_REFLOGIT (logit), B3 = BL3_DIFFMEAN, B7 (resolve the name from score_panel.py BASELINES and print the definition), X2 / X10_abs (weight, parent-free), BL7_JORAK_A (weight), and the shuffled-label band. Recompute each incumbent with iter-2 code and check it against iter-2's stored values (e_tests.json / score outputs). A mismatch above 1e-6 relative STOPS the run until it is reconciled.\\n\\nOUTCOMES (per checkpoint, from judged rows, never from repo names)\\n  HC = harmful-compliance rate (PRIMARY), OR = over-refusal rate, SWC = severity-weighted compliance (if the judged rows carry severity; else NOT_AVAILABLE), all recomputed with Lane C's lc_analyze behavioural_columns aggregation. mlabonne comes from judge_extension.json.\\n  Split-half reliability per outcome: random item halves, 1000 splits, Spearman-Brown corrected, family-clustered. The ATTENUATION CEILING = sqrt(reliability of the outcome) is printed at the head of the table, because it caps every achievable rho.\\n\\nTHE PRIMARY STATISTIC\\n  LOFO rho = the Spearman correlation between the pooled out-of-fold scores and HC over the SELECTION panel. For unfitted candidates (every C except C5 and C14) the out-of-fold score is the raw per-checkpoint value, so LOFO rho equals the plain Spearman. Sign convention: every candidate is pre-oriented in prereg (for example, higher recognition means lower HC), so the reported rho is a signed agreement.\\n  Paired difference Delta = rho(C) - rho(BL1). 95% interval by a FAMILY-cluster bootstrap (resample families with replacement, recompute both rhos on the same draw, B = 10,000, percentile and BCa both printed). With 5 families this interval is very wide; say so in the table header.\\n  Partial rho(C, HC | BL1): rank-based partial Spearman, same bootstrap.\\n\\nSELECTION RULE (copied verbatim into prereg; applied on the SELECTION panel only)\\nA candidate survives if and only if ALL of the following hold:\\n (i) LOFO rho exceeds BL1's by >= 0.15, and the paired family-bootstrap interval for the difference excludes 0;\\n (ii) the partial rho given BL1 has an interval excluding 0;\\n (iii) it lies outside the shuffled-label band. P = 500 permutations of the HARD and EASY harm labels are made WITHIN each checkpoint BEFORE any axis is fitted; the whole candidate pipeline is re-run, the panel rho with HC is recomputed, and the band is the 95th percentile of |rho_perm|;\\n (iv) it moves by less than 1 null-SD on the measured null-edit pair (granite-3.2-2b-instruct to granite-abliterated; judged HC delta 0.000). null-SD is the SD of the candidate's value over 200 item-bootstrap refits inside the CHILD, with prompts resampled by twin pair. [CLAR-3] For the random-initialised Qwen3-0.6B arm, 'does not move' means that its RandInit value lies within 1 null-SD of the value the SAME candidate takes under shuffled labels in that arm; that is, it reads no structure on an untrained network. If a candidate reads a trained-like value on RandInit, it measured the prompts, not the model;\\n (v) it is not at ceiling or floor in any arm (an arm is flagged when > 50% of SELECTION checkpoints sit within 0.02 of the candidate's attainable bound, or it has zero variance).\\nTies go to the candidate needing fewer prompts (C12 = 0 < C1 = 16 < the others). No maximum over k, layer or site is ever taken after looking. If nothing survives, survivor.json = NONE, the ranked table is the result, and no subgroup is searched.\\nMINIMUM DETECTABLE EFFECT, per row: the rho detectable at power 0.80 and two-sided alpha 0.05 at the row's n (Fisher z: n = 16 gives about 0.65; n = 22 gives about 0.57), plus the half-width of the Delta interval. Every row prints both.\\n\\nSAME-EVIDENCE DISCIPLINE\\nEvery candidate and bar is reported twice: on the SELECTION panel (where the rule is applied) and on its full available panel (prompt-site candidates plus C12 over all graded valid tags, with and without the ANOMALOUS rows). No candidate wins by having more rows.\\nSecondary outcomes (OR, SWC) are reported with the same machinery but are DESCRIPTIVE: they do not enter the rule, and Holm correction is applied across the 14 primary-outcome clause-(i) tests only.\\n\\nTHE ACTIVATION-LEVEL THREE-MODEL MAP (the commissioned deliverable, delivered whatever the screen finds)\\nFor Qwen3-4B-Base, Qwen3-4B, Qwen3-4B-SafeRL, mlabonne/Qwen3-4B-abliterated, CohenQu non-safety FT and RandInit-Qwen3-0.6B: a depth x site heat map of cross-fit own-axis d and TPR@5%FPR at the prompt site, response position 0 and response position 1, every layer, plus g(l) (logit-lens drive) as a third panel. Also printed:\\n  - the parent-child axis |cos| per layer (instruct vs abliterated; instruct vs Base; instruct vs SafeRL) at the prompt site and response position 0, beside the granite null-edit pair's same curve as the null. This is the reference for the 0.992-shallow / 0.459-deep result, recomputed.\\n  - every one of the 14 candidates evaluated on these arms, with BL1 beside it.\\nFigures are rendered with the aii-data-fig-gen skill from the saved JSON.\\n\\nDELIVERABLES\\nprereg.json + prereg.sha256; panel.json; asset_ledger.csv; screen_table.json (one row per candidate and bar: READOUT_CLASS, eligible, n, rho [CI], Delta vs BL1 [CI], partial rho [CI], shuffled band, |rho| outside band y/n, null-edit move in null-SD, RandInit move in null-SD, ceiling flag, MDE, prompts needed, pass/fail per clause (i)-(v), rho on the full panel, rho vs OR, rho vs SWC); k_curves.json; per_prompt_scores/ (npz); three_model_map.json + figures; outcome_reliability.json; survivor.json (a candidate id or NONE, sha256, UTC freeze time, prereg sha256 echoed); deviations.json; SCREEN_REPORT.md. The report opens with (a) the corrected panel facts from Step 0, (b) the survivor line, and (c) the sentence that a survivor is a READOUT until the held-out panel and the causal depth-by-site map from this iteration's parallel experiments are joined to it. Emit full / mini / preview JSON variants (aii-json skill) and split any array over 90 MiB into parts from the start (aii-file-size-limit; the deploy gate scans the whole workspace).\\n\\nORDER, TIMING AND CUT ORDER\\n0:00-0:25 Step 0 ledger + panel. 0:25-1:00 optional top-up (hard cap 35 min). 1:00-1:10 prereg frozen. 1:10-2:10 C1-C10, C12, C13 + incumbents + per-prompt scores, then the three-model map. 2:10-2:50 permutations (P = 500; cut to 200 if the per-candidate loop exceeds 90 s) + bootstraps. 2:50-3:20 C5 / C14 LOFO. 3:20-4:20 C11 pass (cap 60 min). 4:20-5:00 survivor freeze, report, figures.\\nNEVER CUT: Step 0, prereg hash, clauses (i)-(v), the RandInit arm, the three-model map, and survivor.json. CUT ORDER: C11 first (to NOT_EVALUATED), then the top-up, then P to 200, then the k = 4 and k = 8 rungs of C1. Every cut is recorded in deviations.json.\\nFAILURE MODES: a file that fails to load is recorded verbatim and its quantity marked ABSENT, never inferred from the file's name. A recomputed incumbent that disagrees with its stored value stops the run until reconciled. Use background runs plus polling for anything longer than a few minutes, and verify PIDs with ps (iteration 2 lost a watcher to an off-by-one $!). Build the environment with uv and a lock file; the iteration-1 causal lane died on a missing .venv.\",\n  \"metrics_justification\": \"WHY A SCREEN ON SAVED ACTIVATIONS, NOT A NEW HARVEST. The iteration-2 harvest already holds all-layer prompt-site activations for 25 checkpoints and response-site activations for 18. Every candidate except C11 is a pure function of those arrays, so all 14 can be scored with ONE measure on ONE body of evidence against ONE bar. That is the only way a comparison of readouts answers the mechanistic question ('at what depth and site does safety behaviour become readable?') rather than reflecting who got the better harvest. The candidates were grouped so that they can disagree: early recognition (C1, C4, C13), the late response site (C2, C9), depth geometry (C3, C6, C7, C8), a drive residual (C5), the benign side (C10), severity grading (C11), weights only (C12), and an early+late combination (C14). Numbers already on disk pull in different directions: recognition rose under safety tuning but barely moved under abliteration, while late-site separability fell under lesion and prompt-site separability did not. Which group wins, or that none does, localises safety in depth and site. That is the study's result, and it is why the screen is framed as one question and not as a detector leaderboard, a lane that is already occupied.\\n\\nWHY BL1 IS THE BAR AND WHY THE CLASS COLUMN IS MANDATORY. The user's invariant is that any metric must read ONE model's activations or weights; logit and text readouts are baselines. The run's history shows why a sentence in the methods is not enough: the final-layer logit gap was the most consistent detector last iteration (6/6 same sign across the effective pairs), and a logit-side quantity has previously been described as the run's winner. So BL1 is the explicit bar, clause (ii) demands information BEYOND BL1 (partial rho), and a required READOUT_CLASS column makes it structurally impossible for a logit or text row to be selected. The intermediate-layer logit-lens drives are classed as activation readouts because they project layer-l residual states; the final-layer value is BL1 itself, which is why C5 regresses it out inside each fold.\\n\\nWHY THESE PANEL CORRECTIONS MATTER. Opening the files during planning showed that the strategy's '25 graded checkpoints' and '18-checkpoint common panel' each included two ungraded rows: CohenQu has behaviour=null, and the random-init arm was never judged. The response-site array also covers 96 teacher-forced request x continuation cells, not the 256 prompts. Applying the rule on 18 would either have crashed on missing outcomes or quietly imputed them. Applying it to response-site features without holding the continuation text fixed would have let a readout 'detect harm' by reading the teacher-forced continuation, which is identical across models. CLAR-2 compares requests at the same continuation, and the RandInit arm is the check that this worked. This run has twice built an iteration on an unverified claim about its own files, so Step 0 counts everything by script before a number is quoted.\\n\\nWHY THE RULE IS STRICT AND WHY THE POWER STATEMENT IS PART OF THE RESULT. With 16 checkpoints in 5 families, a Spearman rho needs to be about 0.65 to be detectable at power 0.8, and a family-cluster bootstrap on the DIFFERENCE from BL1 is wider still. A margin of +0.15 with an interval excluding zero is demanding, and the likely outcome is NONE. That is acceptable only if the table can say what WOULD have been detected. Hence the per-row MDE and the outcome split-half reliability, whose square root caps any achievable rho (last round, the noisiest judge target carried the largest reported margin). The shuffled-label band re-runs the whole pipeline, axis fit included, because the peaks and cross-fits in C4, C8 and C13 can manufacture correlation from label structure alone. In iteration 1, |A| under permuted labels reached 11.0-11.8 null-SD against a real 4.3-9.3 in the trained arms; that is the failure this clause exists to catch. The granite null edit (judged HC delta 0.000) catches readouts that fire on any edited child regardless of behaviour, and the random-init arm catches readouts that measure the prompts instead of the model. The field has documented readouts passing on untrained networks, and iteration 2's Jorak statistic read 0.99 on an UNEDITED parent. Ceiling and floor checks prevent a repeat of iteration 1's saturated AUROC 1.000, which could not falsify anything.\\n\\nWHY FAMILY CLUSTERING, NO MAXIMA AND A HASH FREEZE. Checkpoints within a family share weights and are not independent, so every interval resamples families. Layer, site and k rules are fixed in prereg because a maximum over layers or budgets chosen after looking is how iteration 2 reported the peak of a non-monotonic budget scan as a result. survivor.json is hashed and timestamped without seeing this iteration's held-out panel or causal map, which run in parallel and name no winner. Joining them is then a lookup, not a second look, and it keeps the held-out confirmation single-use.\\n\\nWHY THE THREE-MODEL MAP SHIPS REGARDLESS. The commissioned deliverable is the activation-level comparison of Base, safety-tuned and abliterated Qwen3-4B. The depth x site heat maps with parent-child axis cosines, compared against the granite null-edit curve, show directly where the abliterated model keeps its parent's harm code (the iteration-2 reading is shared shallow, |cos| 0.992, and divergent deep, 0.459) and where safety tuning changes it. Printing all 14 candidates and BL1 on those arms makes the comparison useful even if no candidate generalises across families. The map, not the survivor, is what the paper reports if the screen returns NONE.\"\n}\n", "structuredPatch": [], "originalFile": null, "userModified": false}

P = 500 permutations of the HARD and EASY harm labels are made WITHIN each checkpoint BEFORE any axis is fitted; the whole candidate pipeline is re-run, the panel rho with HC is recomputed, and the band is the 95th percentile of |rho_perm|;\\n (iv) it moves by less than 1 null-SD on the measured null-edit pair (granite-3.2-2b-instruct to granite-abliterated; judged HC delta 0.000). null-SD is the SD of the candidate's value over 200 item-bootstrap refits inside the CHILD, with prompts resampled by twin pair. Build the environment with uv and a lock file; the iteration-1 causal lane died on a missing .venv.\",", "   \"metrics_justification\": \"WHY A SCREEN ON SAVED ACTIVATIONS, NOT A NEW HARVEST. The iteration-2 harvest already holds all-layer prompt-site activations for 25 checkpoints and response-site activations for 18. Every candidate except C11 is a pure function of those arrays, so all 14 can be scored with ONE measure on ONE body of evidence against ONE bar. That is the only way a comparison of readouts answers the mechanistic question ('at what depth and site does safety behaviour become readable?') rather than reflecting who got the better harvest. The candidates were grouped so that they can disagree: early recognition (C1, C4, C13), the late response site (C2, C9), depth geometry (C3, C6, C7, C8), a drive residual (C5), the benign side (C10), severity grading (C11), weights only (C12), and an early+late combination (C14). Numbers already on disk pull in different directions: recognition rose under safety tuning but barely moved under abliteration, while late-site separability fell under lesion and prompt-site separability did not. Which group wins, or that none does, localises safety in depth and site. That is the study's result, and it is why the screen is framed as one question and not as a detector leaderboard, a lane that is already occupied.\\n\\nWHY BL1 IS THE BAR AND WHY THE CLASS COLUMN IS MANDATORY. The user's invariant is that any metric must read ONE model's activations or weights; logit and text readouts are baselines. The run's history shows why a sentence in the methods is not enough: the final-layer logit gap was the most consistent detector last iteration (6/6 same sign across the effective pairs), and a logit-side quantity has previously been described as the run's winner. So BL1 is the explicit bar, clause (ii) demands information BEYOND BL1 (partial rho), and a required READOUT_CLASS column makes it structurally impossible for a logit or text row to be selected. The intermediate-layer logit-lens drives are classed as activation readouts because they project layer-l residual states; the final-layer value is BL1 itself, which is why C5 regresses it out inside each fold.\\n\\nWHY THESE PANEL CORRECTIONS MATTER. Opening the files during planning showed that the strategy's '25 graded checkpoints' and '18-checkpoint common panel' each included two ungraded rows: CohenQu has behaviour=null, and the random-init arm was never judged. The response-site array also covers 96 teacher-forced request x continuation cells, not the 256 prompts. Applying the rule on 18 would either have crashed on missing outcomes or quietly imputed them. Applying it to response-site features without holding the continuation text fixed would have let a readout 'detect harm' by reading the teacher-forced continuation, which is identical across models. CLAR-2 compares requests at the same continuation, and the RandInit arm is the check that this worked. This run has twice built an iteration on an unverified claim about its own files, so Step 0 counts everything by script before a number is quoted.\\n\\nWHY THE RULE IS STRICT AND WHY THE POWER STATEMENT IS PART OF THE RESULT. With 16 checkpoints in 5 families, a Spearman rho needs to be about 0.65 to be detectable at power 0.8, and a family-cluster bootstrap on the DIFFERENCE from BL1 is wider still. A margin of +0.15 with an interval excluding zero is demanding, and the likely outcome is NONE. That is acceptable only if the table can say what WOULD have been detected. Hence the per-row MDE and the outcome split-half reliability, whose square root caps any achievable rho (last round, the noisiest judge target carried the largest reported margin). The shuffled-label band re-runs the whole pipeline, axis fit included, because the peaks and cross-fits in C4, C8 and C13 can manufacture correlation from label structure alone. In iteration 1, |A| under permuted labels reached 11.0-11.8 null-SD against a real 4.3-9.3 in the trained arms; that is the failure this clause exists to catch. The granite null edit (judged HC delta 0.000) catches readouts that fire on any edited child regardless of behaviour, and the random-init arm catches readouts that measure the prompts instead of the model. The field has documented readouts passing on untrained networks, and iteration 2's Jorak statistic read 0.99 on an UNEDITED parent. Ceiling and floor checks prevent a repeat of iteration 1's saturated AUROC 1.000, which could not falsify anything.\\n\\nWHY FAMILY CLUSTERING, NO MAXIMA AND A HASH FREEZE. Checkpoints within a family share weights and are not independent, so every interval resamples families. Layer, site and k rules are fixed in prereg because a maximum over layers or budgets chosen after looking is how iteration 2 reported the peak of a non-monotonic budget scan as a result. survivor.json is hashed and timestamped without seeing this iteration's held-out panel or causal map, which run in parallel and name no winner. Joining them is then a lookup, not a second look, and it keeps the held-out confirmation single-use.\\n\\nWHY THE THREE-MODEL MAP SHIPS REGARDLESS. The commissioned deliverable is the activation-level comparison of Base, safety-tuned and abliterated Qwen3-4B. The depth x site heat maps with parent-child axis cosines, compared against the granite null-edit curve, show directly where the abliterated model keeps its parent's harm code (the iteration-2 reading is shared shallow, |cos| 0.992, and divergent deep, 0.459) and where safety tuning changes it. Printing all 14 candidates and BL1 on those arms makes the comparison useful even if no candidate generalises across families. The map, not the survivor, is what the paper reports if the screen returns NONE.\"", " }"]}], "userModified": false, "replaceAll": false}

### [82] ASSISTANT · 2026-09-21 11:34:18 UTC

```
I've written the plan for evaluation direction 1, the 14-candidate screen, to `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`. The prompt came through cut off, so I rebuilt the task from the iter-3 strategy file and the pipeline's plan schema. I couldn't run a JSON validator here, so the output file hasn't been parse-checked.

**Two things in the strategy were wrong, and the plan corrects them:**
- **Fewer graded checkpoints than claimed.** Only 23 of the 25 harvested checkpoints have judged behaviour, not 25. CohenQu's behaviour is `null` in `pairs_effective.json`, and the random-init model was never judged. The behaviour data sits in Lane C's `judged/*.jsonl` and `judge_extension.json`, not in `scored_checkpoints.json`.
- **The response-site array isn't indexed by the 256 prompts.** `A_resp` covers 96 XSTest cells: 24 scenarios, each with a harmful or benign-twin request and a hazardous or benign scripted reply. So the panel the selection rule runs on is **16 graded checkpoints in 5 families**, not 18. Response-site readouts compare harmful and benign requests with the same reply text, so a readout can't separate them just by reading that text. The random-init model checks this.

**What the plan asks the executor to do:**
- **Count before freezing.** Open and count every file by script, assign each checkpoint a panel status, then hash `prereg.json` before computing any candidate. The 7 missing response-site harvests get a CPU top-up first, capped at 35 minutes; the 4B one took 232 s in iteration 2.
- **Fix every rule in advance.** All 14 candidates have their layer and site rules fixed ahead of time, with the five-clause selection rule copied in word for word. The strategy's 1% false-positive threshold can't be estimated with fewer than 100 benign examples, so the plan uses the zero-false-positive point instead.
- **Enforce your invariant as a column.** Every table row carries a readout-class tag, and only activation or weight rows can win. The final-layer logit gap (BL1) is the bar, and logit and text readouts are marked baseline-only.
- **Say what the panel can detect.** With 16 checkpoints, a correlation must reach about 0.65 to be detectable. The plan asks for this minimum on every row, plus how reliable the judged outcome is. The likely result is no survivor, and the ranked table then stands as the result.
- **Deliver the three-model comparison regardless.** Base, instruct, SafeRL and abliterated Qwen3-4B get depth-by-site maps across three sites (prompt, early reply, late reply). The CohenQu and random-init models are included as controls.

A sibling planner found that iteration 2 mislabelled Lane B's early-reply window as the "prompt site". I checked `harvest.py:130-142` and confirmed this harvest's prompt site really is the last prompt token. The plan still bans the mislabelled +0.0006 / −0.144 site numbers from use.

Resources are declared as cpu_plus, 12 GB RAM, no GPU. I also saved a memory note with these panel corrections.
```
